{"antonyms":[{"word":"little"}],"forms":[{"form":"more","tags":["comparative"]},{"form":"most","tags":["superlative"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"det","senses":[{"examples":[{"text":"Do you think I have much chance of catching the train on time?","type":"example"},{"text":"After much discussion, we decided to set about the task with much enthusiasm.","type":"example"},{"text":"Did you do much running last summer?","type":"example"}],"glosses":["A large amount of."]},{"examples":[{"text":"Add this much water and no more.","type":"example"},{"text":"Take as much time as you like.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Used to indicate, demonstrate or compare the quantity of something."],"qualifier":"'this much'","raw_tags":["in combinations such as 'as much'"]},{"glosses":["A great number of; many (people)."],"tags":["archaic","nonstandard"]},{"glosses":["many ( + plural countable noun)."],"qualifier":"African-American","tags":["Caribbean","UK","regional"]}],"sounds":[{"ipa":"/mʌt͡ʃ/","tags":["Received-Pronunciation"]},{"ipa":"/mʊt͡ʃ/","tags":["Northern-England"]},{"ipa":"/mʌt͡ʃ/","tags":["General-American"]},{"ipa":"/mʌt͡ʃ/","tags":["Canada"]},{"ipa":"/mʊt͡ʃ/","tags":["dialectal"]}],"synonyms":[{"word":"a great deal of"},{"tags":["informal"],"word":"a lot of"}],"upstream_line":191544,"upstream_offset":920808433,"upstream_record_sha256":"b7ca6222ebcc4fe71982b608a196049e46991e55c1d4e2acb4dd2b15ac666c6e","word":"much"}

{"antonyms":[{"sense":"antonym(s) of “to a great extent”","word":"less"},{"sense":"antonym(s) of “to a great extent”","word":"little"},{"sense":"antonym(s) of “to a great extent”","word":"few"},{"sense":"antonym(s) of “to a great extent”","word":"almost"},{"sense":"antonym(s) of “to a great extent”","word":"nearly"}],"forms":[{"form":"more","tags":["comparative"]},{"form":"most","tags":["superlative"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"adv","senses":[{"examples":[{"text":"I don't like melon much, and I don’t much care for milk chocolate either: I find them both much too sweet.","type":"example"},{"text":"However much I eat, I never get fat.","type":"example"},{"text":"He left her, much to the satisfaction of her other suitor.","type":"example"},{"text":"That boyfriend of yours is much like the others.","type":"example"},{"text":"My English was much the worst, and I'm certainly not much good at math either, of that I'm very much aware.","type":"example"},{"text":"Both candidates, who are much of an age, say much the same thing, but the youngest shows much the commoner behavior of the two.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To a great extent."]},{"examples":[{"text":"He is much fatter than I remember him.","type":"example"},{"text":"Honestly, I can't stand much more of this.","type":"example"},{"text":"a much warmer room for much nicer people","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To a great extent.","for emphasis or to indicate a great difference"],"raw_tags":["with a comparative adjective"]},{"examples":[{"text":"Does he get drunk much?","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Often; frequently."]},{"examples":[{"text":"I don't like Wagner as much as I like Mozart.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Used to indicate or compare extent."],"qualifier":"'this much'","raw_tags":["in combinations such as 'as much'"]},{"examples":[{"text":"Jamie's always preaching about how we need to save a planet when she drives literally everywhere she goes. Like, hypocritical much?","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Combining with an adjective or (occasionally) a noun, used in a rhetorical question to mock someone for having the specified quality."],"tags":["slang"]},{"glosses":["Almost."],"tags":["obsolete"]}],"sounds":[{"ipa":"/mʌt͡ʃ/","tags":["Received-Pronunciation"]},{"ipa":"/mʊt͡ʃ/","tags":["Northern-England"]},{"ipa":"/mʌt͡ʃ/","tags":["General-American"]},{"ipa":"/mʌt͡ʃ/","tags":["Canada"]},{"ipa":"/mʊt͡ʃ/","tags":["dialectal"]}],"synonyms":[{"sense":"to a great extent","tags":["informal"],"word":"a great deal"},{"sense":"to a great extent","tags":["informal"],"word":"a lot"},{"sense":"to a great extent","word":"greatly"},{"sense":"to a great extent","word":"highly"},{"sense":"to a great extent","tags":["informal"],"word":"loads"},{"sense":"to a great extent","tags":["US","especially","slang"],"word":"plenty"},{"sense":"to a great extent","word":"very much"}],"upstream_line":191546,"upstream_offset":920856827,"upstream_record_sha256":"17b91a9c375a028e235fa7c80ec09817ed929975ac47bd2812e4958dbbbd1d42","word":"much"}

{"forms":[{"form":"trailers","tags":["plural"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"noun","senses":[{"glosses":["Someone who or something that trails (follows behind); something that is trailing."]},{"examples":[{"text":"the trailer of a plant","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Part of an object which extends some distance beyond the main body of the object."],"synonyms":[{"word":"appendage"},{"word":"attachment"},{"word":"appendix"},{"word":"extension"},{"word":"extrusion"}]},{"examples":[{"text":"At the end of the day, we put the snowmobiles back on the trailer.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["An unpowered wheeled vehicle (not a caravan or camper) that is towed behind another, and used to carry equipment, etc., that cannot be carried in the leading vehicle."]},{"examples":[{"text":"We drove our trailer to Yellowstone Park.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["A furnished vehicle towed behind another, and used as a dwelling when stationary."],"synonyms":[{"word":"camper"},{"word":"caravan"},{"word":"travel trailer"}],"tags":["US"]},{"examples":[{"text":"The young couple′s first home was in a trailer.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["A prefabricated home that could be towed to a new destination but is typically permanently left in an area designated for such homes."],"synonyms":[{"word":"mobile home"}],"tags":["US"]},{"examples":[{"text":"The trailer for that movie makes it seem like it would be fun.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["A preview of a film, video game or TV show."],"synonyms":[{"word":"preview"},{"word":"teaser"}],"tags":["US"],"topics":["media"]},{"glosses":["A short blank segment of film at the end of a reel, for convenient insertion of the film in a projector."]},{"examples":[{"text":"The linked list terminates with a trailer record.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["The final record of a list of data items, often identified by a key field with an otherwise invalid value that sorts last alphabetically (e.g., “ZZZZZ”) or numerically (“99999”);
